B. Other activities 


The Council of Architecture through the Council of Architecture Training and Research Centre 
(COA-TRC) has successfully conducted the following activities: 


e Displayed 40 panels showcasing the heritage and architecture across India at the Bharat 
Parv — an annual event organised at New Delhi as a part of the Republic Day celebrations 
from 26" January 2025 to 3% January 2025. 


[भाग ॥|-खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 67 


e Launched schemes for providing support and facility to teachers, professionals and students 
of the undergraduate and diploma courses in architecture in the form of Indigenous books 
through the Academic Resource Development Scheme (ARDS)-2025 and the Architecture 
Knowledge Repository for publishing books authored by architects. 

e The Sanshodhan 2025 scheme was launched with an aim to develop a research culture 
within the recognized schools of architecture in close collaboration with all stakeholders of 
the built environment. 

e In the Academic year of 2024-25 the COA TRC has worked on conceptualization and 
detailing of the COA International conference titled TRANSFORMATIONS 2025 Co-scripting 
Change with disruptive Technologies in Architecture, Design and Urbanism to be conducted 
in collaboration with D.Y. Patil Deemed to be University, School of Architecture, Navi Mumbai, 
on 26", 27" and 28" June 2025. 


e A series of 2 pre-conference webinars were conducted of which the following 7 were 
conducted up to 34*t March 2025: 


Inauguration of the conference webinar series by the COA President Prof. Abhay Purohit 
followed by Prof. Dr. Sajeev Vidyarthi's talk on Situating the idea of ‘Disruptions’ in the 
fields of architecture and urbanism: Elaborating Theme speakers: Prof. Abhay Purohit 
(President COA), Prof. Dr. Sanjeev Vidyarthi, Prof. Jayashree Deshpande (COA TRC), 
Prof. Aparna Surve (Dean, DYPUSOA) on 06-0-2025

and Gagandeep Bhullar on 3-02-2025

TRAINING FOR UNIVERSAL ACCESSIBILITY AUDITORS : In order to further the objectives of 
the Architects Act, 972, the Council has entered into MOU with Department of Empowerment of 
Persons with Disabilities, Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment, Govt. of India, inter alia 
for preparing a Manual on Universal Accessibility and conduct of training of Architects / Universal 
Accessibility Auditors, incorporating Universal Accessibility as a core subject in the course 
curriculum of Architecture course as all levels. The MOU aimed to promote awareness and 
conduct workshops, seminars, and training programs on making public spaces and buildings 
accessible for Persons with Disabilities (PwDs). It also facilitated the development and delivery of 
certificate courses on universal accessibility for practicing architects, academicians, and faculty 
members. The Council conducted online training programs for Master Trainers for Universal 
Accessibility Auditors, which culminated in a physical training sessions in New Delhi at Dr. 
Ambedkar International Centre, New Delhi in two slots on Ith and 2th March, 2024 as well as 
on 4th and 5th July, 2024. The Master Trainers received certificates jointly issued by the 
President, COA, and Secretary, Department of Empowerment of Persons with Disabilities. The 
Council also completed the drafting of a Comprehensive Manual on Universal Accessibility 
after an extensive consultative process, which included 33 meetings with experts from diverse 
disciplines, including nominated representatives from the Department of Empowerment of 
Persons with Disabilities (DEPwD), Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment. 


LAUNCH OF SANSHODHAN - A RESEARCH SCHEME : The Council, through its Training and 
Research Centre (COA-TRC), Pune, is launching a unique research initiative titled 'संशोधन 
(Sanshodhan)’ in 2025 to promote a robust research culture in architecture across recognized 
institutions. The scheme supports up to 00 research projects annually with grants of up to 
%3,00,000 (extendable to %5,00,000 for exceptional proposals) for a period of two years. It has 
three categories: the Research Mentorship Program for young faculty, Outcome Oriented Research 
Program for senior academicians and architects, and participation by research-oriented 
architectural firms. The program encourages innovative, outcome-driven research in collaboration 
with local stakeholders, with a focus on societal impact. Projects will be selected through a rigorous 
evaluation process, and grants will be disbursed in three installments based on project milestones. 
Research outcomes must include detailed reports and papers. 


LAUNCH OF THE ARCHITECTURE KNOWLEDGE REPOSITORY: The Council of Architecture 
(CoA) has proudly introduced Architecture Knowledge Repository—a visionary platform established 
to document, preserve, and disseminate the wide spectrum of architectural knowledge rooted in 
India’s cultural, social, and environmental diversity. This initiative aims not only to enrich 
architectural education and practice in India but also to contribute meaningfully to the global 
discourse in architecture by showcasing India’s traditions, innovations, and contemporary practices. 


[भाग ॥|-खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 69 


54. 


52. 


53. 


The initiative reflects the Council’s continued commitment to nurturing academically rigorous and 
professionally relevant architectural literature, with a focus on indigenous narratives, critical inquiry, 
and contemporary challenges. The repository will facilitate the generation and circulation of original 
architectural thought, particularly in the areas of India’s architectural heritage, contextual urbanism, 
sustainability, and future-ready design approaches. By offering this dedicated platform, the Council 
seeks to empower educators, students, and professionals with access to credible, high-quality, and 
context-specific publications. The repository also encourages scholarly engagement and 
collaborative knowledge-building, thereby helping to raise the standards of architectural education 
and practice in the country. 

LAUNCH OF ACADEMIC RESOURCE DEVELOPMENT SCHEME (ARDS) : The Council of 
Architecture (CoA) has launched the Study Books Writing & Translation Scheme under the 
Academic Resource Development Scheme (ARDS) 2025 to address the shortage of curriculum- 
aligned and locally relevant study materials in architecture and allied fields, including design, 
construction technology, heritage, sustainability, and emerging technologies. Under this initiative, 
CoA aims to publish up to 00 original and translated books during 2025-26, authored in English, 
Hindi, and regional languages. The scheme invites reputed subject-matter experts with proven 
academic or professional experience to contribute as authors, reviewers, or translators. Authors 
selected under the scheme will receive an honorarium of %7,000 per page, along with additional 
payments for reviewers and translators. Funding will be released in stages, linked to the 
achievement of project milestones. Applications will be evaluated based on subject expertise, 
language proficiency, and prior academic or professional work in the field. 


COUNCIL EXTENDS LOGO SUPPORT TO NATIONAL AND INTERNATIONAL EVENTS: The 
Council has extended its official logo support to several national and international 
academic and professional events aimed at advancing architectural education, awareness, 
and excellence. Through this initiative, organisers of conferences, seminars, workshops, 
exhibitions, and other forums can feature the CoA logo on promotional material after due 
approval. This not only strengthens event credibility but also signifies alignment with the 
Council’s vision of fostering collaboration and professional growth in architecture and 
design. Below is a list of approved events receiving CoA logo support: 


4. APIC (Architecture and People International Conference) — Rizvi College of Architecture, 
Mumbai (Reflections of the Future 2.0 — Architecting Intelligence) 

2. International Conference on Social Sustainability - Faculty of Planning & Architecture, 
Poornima University 

3. International Conference on Sustainable Architecture: Circular Economy — University School 
of Architecture and Planning (USAP) 

4. BAIC Exhibition 2025 — BAIC Exhibition & Events, Maniram Dewan Trade Centre, Guwahati, 
Assam 

5. WADE ASIA 2025 Conference & Awards — Yashobhoomi (IICC), Dwarka, Delhi 

6. International Conference on Social Sustainability - Bharati Vidyapeeth (Deemed to be 
University) 

By extending its logo support, the CoA reaffirms its commitment to encouraging meaningful 

dialogues, strengthening professional ethics, and supporting initiatives that contribute to the 

progress of the architectural community in India and beyond. 


INTERNATIONAL COLLABORATION DIALOGUE IN LOS ANGELES : An informal meeting was 
held on April 24, 2024 in Los Angeles (U.S.A.) with Prof. Abhay V. Purohit, President, Council of 
Architecture, where high-level discussions took place on advancing collaboration between the 
organizations. The deliberations reaffirmed the shared commitment to pursue a Mutual Recognition 
Agreement (MRA) and to continue dialogue on aligning competencies through an updated 
competency matrix. Both sides expressed enthusiasm to move forward, with volunteers proposed 
to support evaluation efforts and future interactions planned through digital platforms.

CONDUCT OF REGISTRATION AND RENEWAL CAMPS BY THE COUNCIL: The Council 
organised Registration/Renewal camps for architects at following cities for work related to 
Registration, Renewal of Registration of Architects: 


e One day Camp at Hamirpur held on 5" May, 2024 

e One day Camp at IIA Jharkhand held on 20" December 2024 

e Two days camp at IIA Kolhapur held on 77 and 8" of January 2025 

e Two days camp at IIA Chennai Centre held on 08" & 09" February 2025. 
COUNCIL’S SOCIAL MEDIA COMPETITION - “THAT ONLY SHOT” : The Council of 
Architecture organized “That Only Shot,” a weekly Instagram photo contest that invited participants 
to capture the essence of architecture through their lens. The competition celebrated the diversity, 
beauty, and creativity of architectural forms while encouraging people to view their surroundings 
with a sharper eye for design and aesthetics. Open to photographers, architecture students, and 
enthusiasts alike, the contest provided a platform to share unique perspectives with a wider 
audience. Each week, a new theme was announced, inspiring participants to showcase 
architectural concepts and elements in fresh and creative ways. Each winning entry was published 
in COA newsletters and the winner was awarder with a commendation certificate and a reward of 
Rs. 3000 along with one book. Through “That Only Shot,” the Council of Architecture fostered a 
deeper appreciation for architectural beauty and highlighted its impact on communities and 
everyday experiences. 
PRESIDENT’S INTERACTION WITH HON’BLE MP, SOUTH MADRAS : The President of the 
Council of Architecture had a cordial interaction with Smt. Thamizhachi Thangapandian, Hon’ble 
Member of Parliament from South Madras, Tamil Nadu, during which matters relating to 
architectural education, professional practice, and the role of architecture in addressing urban and 
community development challenges were discussed. The meeting highlighted the importance of 
collaborative efforts between policymakers and the profession in ensuring sustainable growth and 
preserving cultural identity, with the Hon’ble MP assuring her support for initiatives that strengthen 
architectural practice and education in the region. 
DISCUSSION ON DESIGN HACKATHON FOR YUGA YUGEEN BHARAT MUSEUM : The 
Council held a meeting with Ms. Mugdha Sinha, Joint Secretary (GLAM), Ministry of Culture, on 6 
July 2024, to discuss the possibility of hosting a design hackathon for the upcoming Yuga Yugeen 
Bharat Museum. The proposed initiative aims to engage young architects and designers in 
contributing innovative ideas for the museum’s design, interpretation, and visitor experience, 
thereby fostering creativity and collaboration while showcasing the role of architecture in preserving 
and presenting India’s cultural legacy. 
COA X KWIKPIC: STREAMLINING PHOTO MANAGEMENT WITH Al TECHNOLOGY: The 
Council of Architecture has hired the services of Kwikpic, an advanced photo-sharing and 
management platform, to simplify the process of storing, organizing, and sharing event 
photographs. With Al-powered facial recognition technology (99.9% accuracy) and QR-based 
invitations, the platform ensures seamless, secure, and efficient photo delivery to all members and 
event participants. All media assets are encrypted and securely stored on AWS, ensuring complete 
data ownership and privacy. To preserve and showcase our growing visual archive, COA has also 
launched the “COA Photo Gallery”, integrated with our official website www.coa.gov.in. The gallery 
serves as a centralized digital repository celebrating the Council’s milestones, events, and 
initiatives. Explore it at https://coa.kwikpic.in/ 


59. COA ON SOCIAL MEDIA : The Council of Architecture continues to strengthen its digital presence 


across all major social media platforms. Through these channels, COA actively works towards 
creating awareness among architects about the Council’s initiatives, policies, and e-services, while 
also educating the public about the importance and impact of architecture in everyday life. Regular 
updates on events, conferences, and activities, along with timely notifications regarding NATA and 
PGETA — including important dates, schedules, and preparation tips — have helped COA build a 
strong and trusted digital identity. With a thriving online community of over 63,000 followers on 
Instagram and 25,000 on Facebook, COA’s reach continues to expand, crossing over one million 
views collectively. Social media has also emerged as a dynamic communication channel, allowing 
architects, students, and the public to directly engage with the Council. Through consistent 
interaction and prompt responses, COA ensures accessibility, transparency, and an open line of 
communication with its audience.

Audit Report and Financial Statement for Year: 2024-25 


To, 
The COUNCIL OF ARCHITECTURE 
Report on the Audit of the Financial Statements 


Qualified Opinion 


We have audited the accompanying financial statements of the Council of Architecture (“the Council), 
which comprise the Balance Sheet as at 34st March 2025, the Statement of Income and Expenditure, 
the Statement of Receipt & Payment for the year then ended, and notes to the financial statements, 
including a summary of the significant accounting policies and other explanatory information (herein 
after referred to as “Financial Statement’) 


In our opinion and to the best of our information and according to the explanations given to us, except 
for the effects of the matter described in the Basis for Qualified Opinion section of our report the 
aforesaid financial statement give the information required by the Council of Architecture Regulation, 
972 (as amended up to date) in the manner so required and give a true and fair view in conformity 
with the accounting principles generally accepted in India of the state of affairs of the Council as at 3/st 
March 2025, its surplus and its receipt & payment for the year ended on that date. 


Basis for Qualified Opinion 


We further draw attention to Note No.3 the Council has transferred fund from “General Fund/Surplus/ 
Deficit account" to “House Building Premises Fund". The above fund pertains to the FY:207-8 for 
Rs. 0.57 Cr, for FY:20 8-9 of Rs 4.26 Cr and for FY 209-20 of Rs 8.90 Cr. As per the provisions 
of Income tax act 96/ and rules made thereunder, which states that the earmarked funds should be 


72: THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY [PART III—SECc.4] 


used for the specific purpose only and moreover utilization of the above fund should be made within a 
period of Five years, from the end of the financial year of creation of the fund. During the current year, 
the funds for the FY: 209-20 is not utilized up to the end of financial year, due to which it should also 
be treated as the Taxable Income of the Council for current financial year, in respect of the fund created 
in the FY:20 7-8,utilization was required to be made on or before 37.03.2023. due to this same 
should be treated as taxable income of FY 2022-23 and in respect of the fund created in the FY:208- 
9, utilization was required to be made on or before 34.03.2024. due to this same should be treated 
as taxable income of FY 2023-24 but same is not offered for tax in FY 2023-24. Due to this total income 
of the Council for current financial year is understated by 43.73 Cr and House Building Premises Fund 
is overstated by same figure, however the Council is consistently making efforts for utilisation of fund 
and made application for allowing extension of time for utilisation of fund for FY 20i7-8 and FY 208- 
9 to Chairman,CBDT, which is yet to be disposed of by income tax department. 


We conducted our audit in accordance with the Standards on Auditing (SAs) issued by the Institute of 
Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI). Our responsibilities under those Standards are further 
described in the Auditor’s Responsibilities for the Audit of the Financial Statements section of our 
report. We are independent of the Council in accordance with the ethical requirements that are relevant 
to our audit of the financial statements and we have fulfilled our ethical responsibilities in accordance 
with these requirements and the Code of Ethics. We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained 
is sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion. 


Responsibilities of Management for the Financial Statements 


The Council’s Management is responsible for the preparation of these financial statements that give a 
true and fair view of the financial position, financial performance the Council. This responsibility also 
includes maintenance of adequate accounting records in accordance with the provisions of the Act for 
safeguarding the assets of the Council and for preventing and detecting frauds and other irregularities; 
selection, application of appropriate accounting policies; making judgements and estimates that are 
reasonable and prudent; and design, implementation and maintenance of adequate internal financial 
controls, that were operating effectively for ensuring the accuracy and completeness of the accounting 
records, relevant to the preparation and presentation of the financial statements that give a true and 
fair view and are free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error. 


In preparing the financial statements, the management is responsible for assessing the Council’s 
ability to continue as a going concern, disclosing, as applicable, matters related to going concern and 
using the going concern basis of accounting unless management either intends to liquidate the Council 
or to cease operations, or has no realistic alternative but to do so. Those charged with governance 
are also responsible for overseeing the Council's financial reporting process. 


Auditor’s Responsibilities for the Audit of the Financial Statements 


Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the financial statements as a whole 
are free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error, and to issue an auditor’s report 
that includes our opinion. Reasonable assurance is a high level of assurance, but is not a guarantee 
that an audit conducted in accordance with SAs will always detect a material misstatement when it 
exists. Misstatements can arise from fraud or error and are considered material if, individually or in the 
aggregate, they could reasonably be expected to influence the economic decisions of users taken on 
the basis of these financial statements. 


[भाग ॥|-खण्ड 4] भारत का राजपत्र : असाधारण 73 


As part of an audit in accordance with SAs, we exercise professional judgment and maintain 
professional skepticism throughout the audit. We also: 


¢ Identify and assess the risks of material misstatement of the financial statements, whether due 
to fraud or error, design and perform audit procedures responsive to those risks, and obtain audit 
evidence that is sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion. The risk of not 
detecting a material misstatement resulting from fraud is higher than for one resulting from error, 
as fraud may involve collusion, forgery, intentional omissions, misrepresentations, or the override 
of internal controls. 


¢ Obtain an understanding of internal controls relevant to the audit in order to design audit 
procedures that are appropriate in the circumstances, but not for the purpose of expressing an 
opinion on the effectiveness of the Council’s internal controls. 


¢ Evaluate the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of accounting 
estimates and related disclosures made by management. 


* Conclude on the appropriateness of the management’s use of the going concern basis of 
accounting and, based on the audit evidence obtained, whether a material uncertainty exists 
related to events or conditions that may cast significant doubt on the Council’s ability to continue 
as a going concern. If we conclude that a material uncertainty exists, we are required to draw 
attention in our auditor's report to the related disclosures in the financial statements or, if such 
disclosures are inadequate, to modify our opinion. Our conclusions are based on the audit evidence 
obtained up to the date of our auditor's report. However, future events or conditions may cause the 
Council to cease to continue as a going concern. 


¢ Evaluate the overall presentation, structure and content of the financial statements, including the 


disclosures, and whether the financial statements represent the underlying transactions and events 
in a manner that achieve fair presentation. 


* We communicate with those charged with governance regarding, among other matters, the 
planned scope and timing of the audit and significant audit findings, including any significant 
deficiencies in internal controls that we identify during our audit. 


¢ We also provide those charged with governance with a statement that we have complied with 
relevant ethical requirements regarding independence, and to communicate with them all 
relationships and other matters that may reasonably be thought to bear on our independence, and 
where applicable, related safeguards.
